    47	// A recording fake git. `responses` maps an args-substring (longest match wins)
    48	// to a canned result; { reject:true } models a nonzero git exit.
    49	function fakeGit(responses = {}) {
    50	  const keys = Object.keys(responses).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length);
    51	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
    52	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
    53	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
    54	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd });
    55	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
    56	    const k = keys.find((key) => argstr.includes(key));
    57	    const r = k ? responses[k] : null;
    58	    if (r?.reject) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(r.stderr || 'git failed'), { code: r.code ?? 1, stdout: r.stdout ?? '', stderr: r.stderr ?? '' }));
    59	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: r?.stdout ?? '', stderr: r?.stderr ?? '' });
    60	  };
    61	  fn.calls = calls;
    62	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
    63	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
    64	  return fn;
    65	}
    66	
    67	// git worktree list --porcelain output for the given seats.
    68	function porcelain(entries) {
    69	  return entries
    70	    .map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${e.sha ?? MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`)
    71	    .join('\n');
    72	}
    73	
    74	function makeRepo({ git, files = {} } = {}) {
    75	  const io = makeIo({
    76	    now: '2026-07-19T00:00:00.000Z',
    77	    host: 'wt-host',
    78	    pid: 4242,
    79	    startTime: 111000,
    80	    files: { '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n', ...files },
    81	  });
    82	  if (git) io.execFile = git;
    83	  io.__git = git;
    84	  return io;
    85	}

   303	  it('a merge CONFLICT aborts (git merge --abort) and parks — never auto-resolved', async () => {
   304	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   305	    const git = fakeGit({
   306	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   307	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { reject: true, code: 1, stdout: 'CONFLICT (content): Merge conflict in src/x.js\nAutomatic merge failed' },
   308	    });
   309	    const io = makeRepo({ git });
   310	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   311	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   312	    assert.ok(git.issued(/merge --abort/), 'the conflicted merge is aborted');
   313	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /conflict|park/i, 'the result is a park-shaped refusal');
   314	    assert.ok(!git.issued(/commit|-X (ours|theirs)|checkout --theirs/), 'no auto-resolution was attempted');
   315	  });
   316	
   317	  it('a clean merge succeeds and fast-forwards BOTH worktrees ff-only', async () => {
   318	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   319	    const git = fakeGit({
   320	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   321	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { stdout: 'Updating; Fast-forward' },
   322	      'merge --ff-only': { stdout: 'Already up to date.' },
   323	    });
   324	    const io = makeRepo({ git });
   325	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   326	    assert.equal(r.ok, true);
   327	    assert.equal(git.git().filter((c) => /merge --ff-only/.test(c.argstr)).length, 2, 'both worktrees fast-forward ff-only after the merge');
   328	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/loop/merge.lock'), 'the merge lock is released after a successful merge');
   329	  });
   330	
   331	  it('a NON-fast-forward worktree sync is a corruption signal → refusal', async () => {
   332	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   333	    const git = fakeGit({
   334	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   335	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { stdout: 'Fast-forward' },
   336	      'merge --ff-only': { reject: true, code: 1, stderr: 'fatal: Not possible to fast-forward, aborting.' },
   337	    });
   338	    const io = makeRepo({ git });
   339	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   340	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   341	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /fast-forward|ff|corrupt|sync/i, 'a non-ff sync refuses as corruption');
   342	  });
   343	
   344	  it('an attribution violation BLOCKS the merge before any git merge', async () => {
   345	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   346	    const bad = logLine('badc0de', 'Someone Else', 'x@y.z', NAME, EMAIL, 'sneaky');
   347	    const git = fakeGit({ [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: bad } });
   348	    const io = makeRepo({ git });
   349	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   350	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   351	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /author|attribution|identity/i, 'the merge is gated on the attribution scan');
   352	    assert.ok(!git.issued(/^merge /), 'no merge is issued when attribution fails');
   353	  });
   354	});
   355	
   356	// ===========================================================================
   357	describe('worktrees — self-heal and teardown', () => {
   358	  it('a raw-deleted worktree dir (listed by git, missing on fs) is pruned then re-added', async () => {
   359	    const { selfHealWorktree } = M();
   360	    const git = fakeGit({
   361	      'worktree list': { stdout: porcelain([{ path: '/repo', branch: 'main' }, { path: '/repo/.worktrees/wt-a', branch: 'baton/wt-a/base' }]) },
   362	    });

   366	    assert.equal(r.healed, true);
   367	    assert.ok(git.issued(/worktree prune/), 'the stale metadata is pruned');
   368	    assert.ok(git.issued(/worktree add/), 'the worktree is re-added');
   369	  });
   370	
   371	  it('teardown removes worktrees + deletes ONLY baton/wt- branches, never a non-baton branch', async () => {
   372	    const { teardownWorktrees } = M();

   374	      'worktree list': {
   375	        stdout: porcelain([

   377	          { path: '/repo/.worktrees/wt-a', branch: 'baton/wt-a/base' },

   380	        ]),
   381	      },
   382	    });

   386	    const deleted = git.git().filter((c) => /branch -D/.test(c.argstr)).map((c) => c.argstr);
   387	    assert.ok(deleted.every((s) => /baton\/wt-(a|b)\//.test(s)), 'only namespaced branches are deleted');
   388	    assert.ok(!deleted.some((s) => /feature\/keep-me/.test(s)), 'a non-baton branch is never deleted');
   389	    assert.ok(!git.issued(/worktree remove [^\n]*other/), 'a non-baton worktree is never removed');
   390	  });
   391	});

/bin/zsh -lc 'nl -ba tests/helpers/fakeio.mjs' in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
    35	} = {}) {
    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    37	  const outChunks = [];
    38	  const errChunks = [];
    39	  let currentNow = now;
    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    42	
    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    45	
    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    52	        return reject(e);
    53	      }
    54	      const r = execResults[key];
    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    58	      if (code !== 0) {
    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    61	        e.code = code;
    62	        e.stdout = out;
    63	        e.stderr = err;
    64	        return reject(e);
    65	      }
    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    67	    });
    68	  };
    69	
    70	  return {
    71	    cwd: '/repo',
    72	    env,
    73	    stdin,
    74	    stdout,
    75	    stderr,
    76	    fs,
    77	    execFile,
    78	    now: () => currentNow,
    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    80	    execPath,
    81	    host,
    82	    pid,
    83	    startTime,
    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
    92	  };
    93	}

docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:19:7. **Attribution (hard invariant)**: ZERO AI mentions in git history — no `Co-Authored-By`, no "Generated with Claude Code" — permanently. During initial construction (pre-v1), all commits are authored solely by `SakshamUboweja <ssakshamu@gmail.com>` with frequent, regular commits to main (contributions accrue to his GitHub profile); external human OSS contributors are welcome post-v1. docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:54:**CLI surface** (`baton <cmd> --json`): `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover` (forced lock recovery). Frozen exit-code contract: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json` mode prints exactly one envelope `{ok, data, warnings, error}` on stdout. `baton wrap -- <cmd>` (PTY supervisor) is **deferred post-v1** — hook-driven detection suffices; the name/contract is reserved.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:58:**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:60:**Journal rotation & crash recovery** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): on every finalize, crash-safe order = write a rotation marker → write frozen snapshot to `history/` → rename `journal.ndjson` alongside it → start a fresh journal seeded with the snapshot's `journalSeq` → clear marker. On load, a present marker deterministically rolls the rotation forward (if the snapshot landed) or back (if not); the same marker pattern governs interrupted forced takeovers and interrupted purges (purge resumes from its marker until the whole tree is clean). Retention: last 10 rotations pruned with the history. Doctor warns at journal > 5 MB; perf test: 10k-event replay < 1 s. **Filesystem support boundary: v1 supports local filesystems only** — lock and rename atomicity are not guaranteed on network/shared mounts (NFS/SMB/cloud-sync folders); doctor detects and warns, README states it.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:62:**Root discovery & degraded modes** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): repo root = nearest ancestor containing `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree-aware: linked worktrees resolve to their own toplevel, shared state stays per-worktree), else cwd — `--root <path>` overrides; nested/monorepo launches therefore resolve to the same bundle deterministically, but **discovery never crosses the boundary of a nested independent git repository** (a `.git` between cwd and a candidate root stops the walk) unless `--root` says otherwise. Non-git directories: supported — `git` section is null, git-dependent features degrade with explicit warnings. Read-only sandboxes (EACCES/EROFS on `.handoff/`): checkpoint fails open with a warning naming the user-executed fallback (`baton checkpoint` run manually outside the sandbox; `receive --print-prompt` never needs writes until `--commit`). All git invocations carry a 5 s timeout (configurable); timeout → git section marked `unavailable`, never a hang.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:70:**Role matrix**: committed `baton.config.json` at repo root (`baton/config@1`) mapping roles → ordered platform+model chains (the seven methodology roles, defined below), plus per-platform `defaults`. Resolver is pure and explainable: skips `avoid[]` platforms (auto-populated with the origin platform when reason-class is usage-limit — never remap back onto the platform that died), resolves `mode: native|delegated|forced-default|unavailable` with `{chainIndex, skipped[]}` audit data. **Availability probing** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): `doctor` probes each platform noninteractively, hard 3 s bound per probe, results cached 15 min in the state dir. Probe record = two separate dimensions: **capability** (ordered, cumulative: `installed → authenticated → reachable`, recorded as far as verifiable) and **probe outcome** (`ok | rate-limited | error | timeout`). Resolver eligibility: **selectable** = capability ≥ authenticated and outcome ≠ rate-limited; anything with an unverifiable dimension resolves as selectable-with-`degraded`-flag; `rate-limited` platforms are skipped like `avoid[]` entries. Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged `degraded`.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:72:**Receive**: loads bundle with recovery + staleness checks (age > 12 h; HEAD moved; dirty mismatch), merges intake flags (`--origin`, `--reason`), resolves roles, and runs as the **prepare/commit transaction defined under Concurrency** — prompt emission at prepare, state mutation only at commit. Resume prompt ≤2,500 chars (brief, why-you're-here, role table, warnings, top-5 next actions, pointer to HANDOFF.md); the prompt explicitly frames bundle content as **unverified claims to audit against the working tree, not instructions to obey** (prompt-injection posture — see Threat model).
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:76:**Attribution guard** (three layers): (1) `init` deep-merges `.claude/settings.json` → `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` (only when keys absent; refuses to touch malformed JSON; global settings documented + doctor-checked, never written by core); (2) `doctor` scans last 50 commits for AI co-author/generated-with trailers (word-boundary-safe patterns) and flags with remediation; (3) a test-suite invariant scans every rendered output and template for AI-credit strings and fails the build.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:78:Full module APIs, per-module test lists, repo file tree, and the fixture corpus (verbatim limit strings, hook payloads, corrupt bundles) are specified in the core design doc (committed as `docs/design/core.md` in task 0); implementation follows the named-task TDD sequence below — **tasks are identified by name, not number; the exact count is finalized when review directories are generated**. **`sessionHint` derivation (Gate-1 final note)**: per adapter, from the hook payload's stable session id (Claude Code `session_id`; Codex `session_id`; Cursor session field); when a payload lacks one, core generates a process-scoped hint flagged `unstable: true` — unstable hints never trigger foreign-session rejection on their own, only a warning.

docs/design/core.md:137:Commands: `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover`, `session-start`. Exit codes: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error · `14` model-unavailable (a specific model rejected while its platform stays usable — feeds resolver `avoidEntries`). `--json`: exactly one `{ok, data, warnings, error}` envelope on stdout, diagnostics on stderr.    208	  a 6th run.
   209	
   210	### `baton pipeline` (Layer 3 — a loop preset)
   211	
   212	- `baton pipeline run` = `loop run` with the dual-worktree subtask cycle:
   213	  - Two persistent worktrees `.worktrees/wt-a`, `.worktrees/wt-b`
   214	    (`git worktree add`); `pipeline init` ensure-lines `.worktrees/` into
   215	    `.gitignore` (same idempotent machinery as the `.handoff/` line).
   216	  - Subtask N: the writer seat implements on a namespaced branch
   217	    (`baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N`) in its worktree; the reviewer seat reviews
   218	    the diff from the OTHER worktree (fresh checkout, fresh context). Seats
   219	    swap every subtask: worker-a writes / worker-b reviews → worker-b writes /
   220	    worker-a reviews → … (concrete models per seat come from the role
   221	    matrix — see Role-matrix additions).
   222	  - Verdict flow: APPROVED → the merger role adversarially re-checks and
   223	    merges into main (the ONLY writer to main); BLOCKED → writer fixes,
   224	    ≤5 iterations, then escalate. After each merge both worktrees
   225	    fast-forward.
   226	
   227	  **Worktree transaction layer (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 5)** —
   228	  `worktrees.mjs` treats every git operation as a guarded transaction:
   229	  - Branch namespace `baton/wt-*/…` is loop-owned; anything else is never
   230	    touched.
   231	  - Preflight before each child: worktree exists in `git worktree list`,
   232	    index clean, HEAD on the expected branch — a dirty or drifted worktree
   233	    parks the subtask instead of spawning.
   234	  - Postflight after each child: HEAD still on the expected branch, main
   235	    unmoved by the child — violation parks with the evidence.
   236	  - Merges run under a supervisor-held merge lock; worktree sync after merge
   237	    is ff-only (a non-ff sync is a corruption signal → park); merge conflicts
   238	    park + escalate, never auto-resolved.
   239	  - Stale metadata self-heals: a raw-deleted worktree directory is detected
   240	    (`git worktree list` vs fs), pruned (`git worktree prune`), and re-added;
   241	    teardown uses `git worktree remove` + branch deletion so acceptance
   242	    constraint 5 (no residue) holds.
   243	  - Adversarial tests: stale branch from a previous run, dirty worker,
   244	    main moved mid-subtask, merge conflict, raw-deleted `.worktrees/`,
   245	    untracked `.worktrees/` (missing gitignore line → doctor check).
   246	
   247	  **Attribution enforcement for child commits (Gate-1 iteration 1,
   248	  finding 6)** — the invariant is enforced, not assumed:
   249	  - The supervisor sets `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL` and `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL`
   250	    (sole author identity) in EVERY child's environment.
   251	  - Every merge is gated on an attribution scan over the exact range
   252	    `main..baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N` — author/committer identity plus the
   253	    forbidden-trailer patterns (the existing doctor patterns, applied to the
   254	    full range, not a last-50 window). A violation blocks the merge and
   255	    parks.
   256	  - The existing doctor 50-commit scan remains the repo-wide backstop.
   257	
   258	### Module layout (all under `core/src/loop/`)
   259	
   260	`spec.mjs` (load/validate loop.json incl. role-existence check) · `state.mjs`
   261	(state machine + journal + smoke-approval token) · `children.mjs` (full child
   262	lifecycle: spawn/process-group/timeout/log-cap, region-bounded verdict parse,
   263	detect-on-exit) · `failover.mjs` (the limit-death transaction) ·
   264	`worktrees.mjs` (guarded git transactions, branch discipline, attribution
   265	range scan) · `commands/loop.mjs`, `commands/pipeline.mjs`. Plus three Layer-1
   266	extensions: `roles/resolve.mjs` gains `avoidEntries[]` (entry-level
   267	avoidance, backward-compatible — omitted means current behavior); the
   268	signature table gains the `model-unavailable` class; `checkpoint` gains a
   269	`--session <hint>` flag (supervisor identity), and the
   270	`BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` no-op guard lands in the hook entrypoints,
   271	`checkpoint`, AND `session-start` (every hook-invoked command).
   272	
   273	### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
   274	
   275	1. On a fixture repo, `baton loop run` drives a toy goal end-to-end headlessly:
   276	   plan → gate-1 → smoke slice AS A TDD MINI-CYCLE (failing smoke assertion
   277	   authored and verifier-approved BEFORE the slice implementation — order
   278	   asserted from the journal) → parked for approval → (approved) → 2 subtasks
   279	   TDD → gate-2 → done, with review artifacts under `reviews/` in the fixture.
   280	   Layer-2 children run in the main root with live hooks and neither mutate
   281	   NOR read the supervisor-owned bundle: under `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1`,
   282	   hook commands on all three platforms produce zero bundle writes AND zero
   283	   session-start resume context (both asserted).
   284	2. Simulated limit death (child exits with a verbatim limit banner) mid-subtask
   285	   → the loop seals, remaps the role off the dead platform, resumes on the
   286	   other harness, and completes the subtask. Chained A→B→A tested. Separately:
   287	   a model-unavailable rejection at spawn selects the NEXT entry on the same
   288	   platform (no platform-wide avoidance).
   289	3. `baton pipeline run` completes 2 subtasks with alternating writer/reviewer
   290	   across the two worktrees; the attribution range scan passes for main AND
   291	   both worker branch ranges (child commits included) — sole author, zero AI
   292	   attribution; reviewer children run read-only.
   293	4. The 5-cap parks + escalates on every gate (unit-forced exhaustion test);
   294	   no gate ever runs a 6th iteration. Killing the supervisor (and its
   295	   launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   296	   journal and continues or parks, with no orphaned children.
   297	5. Deleting `.handoff/` and `.worktrees/` leaves the fixture repo exactly as
   298	   a plain git repo (no residue outside gitignored trees).
   299	6. All existing 878 tests stay green; the new suite covers spec validation,
   300	   state-machine transitions (unit, injected io), child verdict parsing
   301	   (fixture transcripts), failover (fake children), and worktree discipline.

   304	
   305	1. **Headless permission surfaces** — Claude Code children need permission

    15	// Every review seat is read-only — writers are the implementer-side roles only.
    16	const REVIEWER_ROLES = new Set(['plan-reviewer', 'test-verifier', 'subtask-reviewer', 'final-reviewer-a', 'final-reviewer-b']);
    17	
    18	// AGENTS.md §9: children commit solely as the repo owner, zero AI attribution.
    19	const GIT_IDENTITY = Object.freeze({
    20	  GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',
    21	  GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com',
    22	  GIT_COMMITTER_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',
    23	  GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com',
    24	});

    26	/**
    27	 * Assemble the spawn contract for a role child. Reviewers are read-only on
    28	 * every platform; every child is marked supervised (its own baton hooks
    29	 * no-op), carries the sole-author git identity, and has stdin CLOSED at
    30	 * spawn (the codex-exec stdin-hang field failure).
    31	 * @param {{platform: string, role: string, model: string, effort?: string | null, mode?: string}} assignment
    32	 * @param {string} prompt
    33	 * @param {{root: string}} opts
    34	 * @returns {{command: string, args: string[], env: Record<string, string>, stdio: any[]}}
    35	 */
    36	export function buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, opts) {
    37	  const readOnly = REVIEWER_ROLES.has(assignment.role);
    38	  const env = { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1', ...GIT_IDENTITY };
    39	  const stdio = ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'];
    40	
    41	  if (assignment.platform === 'claude-code') {
    42	    const args = ['-p', prompt];
    43	    if (readOnly) {
    44	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'default', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob');
    45	    } else {
    46	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'acceptEdits', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob,Write,Edit,Bash');
    47	    }
    48	    return { command: 'claude', args, env, stdio };
    49	  }
    50	
    51	  // codex (and the default shape for exec-style CLIs).
    52	  const args = ['exec', '-C', opts.root, '-s', readOnly ? 'read-only' : 'workspace-write', '--model', assignment.model];
    53	  if (assignment.effort) args.push('-c', `model_reasoning_effort=${assignment.effort}`);
    54	  args.push(prompt);
    55	  return { command: 'codex', args, env, stdio };
    56	}
    57	
    58	const VERDICT_RE = /VERDICT:\s*(APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|APPROVED|BLOCKED)\b/g;
    59	
    60	/** @param {string} region @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string} | null} */
    61	function lastVerdictIn(region) {
    62	  let last = null;
    63	  for (const m of region.matchAll(VERDICT_RE)) last = m;
    64	  if (!last) return null;
    65	  const after = region.slice((last.index ?? 0) + last[0].length);
    66	  return { verdict: last[1], findings: after.trim() };
    67	}
    68	
    69	/**
    70	 * Region-bounded verdict parsing — the prompt region (which quotes the
    71	 * verdict format in its instructions) is NEVER scanned, and there is no
    72	 * whole-transcript fallback. Unparseable is BLOCKED, never APPROVED.
    73	 * - codex: only the text after the LAST line starting with 'tokens used'.
    74	 * - claude-code: only the `result` field of the structured JSON output.
    75	 * Multiple tails inside the trusted region → the LAST one wins.
    76	 * @param {string} transcript @param {{platform: string}} opts
    77	 * @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string, reason?: string}}
    78	 */
    79	export function parseVerdict(transcript, { platform }) {
    80	  const unparseable = { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: '', reason: 'unparseable' };
    81	  const text = String(transcript ?? '');
    82	
    83	  if (platform === 'claude-code') {
    84	    let obj = null;
    85	    try {
    86	      obj = JSON.parse(text.trim());
    87	    } catch {
    88	      return unparseable;
    89	    }
    90	    if (typeof obj?.result !== 'string') return unparseable;
    91	    const hit = lastVerdictIn(obj.result);
    92	    return hit ?? unparseable;
    93	  }
    94	
    95	  // codex: locate the LAST 'tokens used' marker line; the trusted region is
    96	  // strictly after it.
    97	  const lines = text.split('\n');
    98	  let markerLine = -1;
    99	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i += 1) {
   100	    if (/^tokens used\b/i.test(lines[i])) markerLine = i;
   101	  }
   102	  if (markerLine === -1) return unparseable;
   103	  const region = lines.slice(markerLine + 1).join('\n');
   104	  const hit = lastVerdictIn(region);
   105	  return hit ?? unparseable;
   106	}
   107	
   108	const TRUNCATION_MARKER = '\n[... truncated by baton log cap ...]\n';
   109	
   110	/**
   111	 * Cap a transcript: head preserved, middle replaced with a marker, TAIL
   112	 * preserved verbatim (the verdict lives at the end and must never be cut).
   113	 * @param {string} text @param {number} maxBytes
   114	 * @returns {string}
   115	 */
   116	export function capLog(text, maxBytes) {
   117	  const s = String(text ?? '');
   118	  if (Buffer.byteLength(s) <= maxBytes) return s;
   119	  const headBytes = Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxBytes * 0.4));
   120	  const tailBytes = Math.max(1, maxBytes - headBytes);
   121	  const buf = Buffer.from(s);
   122	  const head = buf.subarray(0, headBytes).toString();
   123	  const tail = buf.subarray(buf.length - tailBytes).toString();
   124	  return head + TRUNCATION_MARKER + tail;
   125	}
   126	
   127	/**
   128	 * Exit classification delegates to core detect — one classifier, one
   129	 * signature table, no drift. Returns exactly classify(...).class.
   130	 * @param {string} transcript @param {number} exitCode @param {string} platform @param {any} table
   131	 * @returns {string}
   132	 */
   133	export function classifyChildExit(transcript, exitCode, platform, table) {
   134	  return classify({ text: transcript, exitCode, platform, table }).class;
   135	}

   137	/** @param {number} pid @param {NodeJS.Signals} sig */
   138	function killGroup(pid, sig) {
   139	  try {
   140	    process.kill(-pid, sig);
   141	  } catch {
   142	    // ESRCH — already gone.
   143	  }
   144	}
   145	
   146	/**
   147	 * Spawn and supervise one child: detached in its OWN process group, stdin
   148	 * closed; on timeout SIGTERM the group, wait graceMs, SIGKILL the group (no
   149	 * zombie grandchildren). Output streams to a capped log at opts.logPath; the
   150	 * verdict is parsed from the full transcript, region-bounded.
   151	 * @param {{command: string, args: string[], env?: Record<string, string>, cwd?: string}} spec
   152	 * @param {{timeoutMs: number, graceMs: number, logPath: string, maxLogBytes?: number, platform?: string}} opts
   153	 * @returns {Promise<{timedOut: boolean, exitCode: number | null, verdict?: string, findings?: string, reason?: string, logPath: string}>}
   154	 */
   155	export function superviseChild(spec, opts) {
   156	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
   157	    const maxLogBytes = opts.maxLogBytes ?? 1_000_000;
   158	    const child = spawn(spec.command, spec.args, {
   159	      cwd: spec.cwd,
   160	      env: { ...process.env, ...(spec.env ?? {}) },
   161	      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
   162	      detached: true, // its own process group — the kill unit
   163	    });
   164	
   165	    let out = '';
   166	    let timedOut = false;
   167	    /** @type {NodeJS.Timeout | null} */
   168	    let killTimer = null;
   169	
   170	    const onChunk = (/** @type {Buffer} */ c) => {
   171	      out += c.toString();
   172	    };
   173	    child.stdout?.on('data', onChunk);
   174	    child.stderr?.on('data', onChunk);
   175	
   176	    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
   177	      timedOut = true;
   178	      if (typeof child.pid === 'number') {
   179	        killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGTERM');
   180	        killTimer = setTimeout(() => {

   295	// Attribution / AI-credit PHRASES. Deliberately narrow so it never trips on

   298	const ATTRIBUTION_PATTERNS = [

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba tests/helpers/memfs.mjs | sed -n '1,240p'" in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// In-memory fs fake for unit tests. Synchronous subset only.
     2	// Atomicity contract: renameSync moves content in a single step, so a target
     3	// path is never observed carrying partial content. Every mutation appends a
     4	// full snapshot to `fs.__history`, letting tests enumerate intermediate states
     5	// and prove the tmp+rename discipline (target's first appearance is a rename).
     6	
     7	function fsError(code, syscall, path) {
     8	  const e = new Error(`${code}: ${syscall} '${path}'`);
     9	  e.code = code;
    10	  e.syscall = syscall;
    11	  e.path = path;
    12	  return e;
    13	}
    14	
    15	function normalize(p) {
    16	  const str = String(p);
    17	  const isAbs = str.startsWith('/');
    18	  const out = [];
    19	  for (const part of str.split('/')) {
    20	    if (part === '' || part === '.') continue;
    21	    if (part === '..') {
    22	      out.pop();
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    out.push(part);
    26	  }
    27	  return (isAbs ? '/' : '') + out.join('/');
    28	}
    29	
    30	function dirname(np) {
    31	  const i = np.lastIndexOf('/');
    32	  if (i <= 0) return '/';
    33	  return np.slice(0, i);
    34	}
    35	
    36	function basename(np, parent) {
    37	  return parent === '/' ? np.slice(1) : np.slice(parent.length + 1);
    38	}
    39	
    40	export function makeMemfs(initialFiles = {}) {
    41	  const store = new Map(); // path -> string content
    42	  const dirs = new Set(['/']);
    43	  const mtimes = new Map();
    44	  const history = [];
    45	  let clock = 0;
    46	
    47	  const snapshot = () => Object.fromEntries(store);
    48	  const record = (op, paths) => history.push({ op, paths, files: snapshot() });
    49	  const touch = (np) => mtimes.set(np, ++clock);
    50	
    51	  const mkdirp = (np) => {
    52	    let cur = '';
    53	    for (const part of np.split('/').filter(Boolean)) {
    54	      cur = cur + '/' + part;
    55	      dirs.add(cur);
    56	    }
    57	  };
    58	
    59	  const ensureParent = (np, syscall) => {
    60	    const parent = dirname(np);
    61	    if (parent === '/' ) return;
    62	    if (!dirs.has(parent)) throw fsError('ENOENT', syscall, np);
    63	  };
    64	
    65	  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(initialFiles)) {
    66	    const np = normalize(p);
    67	    mkdirp(dirname(np));
    68	    store.set(np, String(content));
    69	    touch(np);
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  const fs = {
    73	    readFileSync(p /*, enc */) {
    74	      const np = normalize(p);
    75	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'open', np);
    76	      return store.get(np);
    77	    },
    78	
    79	    writeFileSync(p, data /*, enc */) {
    80	      const np = normalize(p);
    81	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    82	      store.set(np, String(data));
    83	      touch(np);
    84	      record('writeFileSync', [np]);
    85	    },
    86	
    87	    appendFileSync(p, data) {
    88	      const np = normalize(p);
    89	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    90	      store.set(np, (store.get(np) || '') + String(data));
    91	      touch(np);
    92	      record('appendFileSync', [np]);
    93	    },
    94	
    95	    renameSync(from, to) {
    96	      const nf = normalize(from);
    97	      const nt = normalize(to);
    98	      if (store.has(nf)) {
    99	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   100	        const content = store.get(nf);
   101	        store.delete(nf);
   102	        store.set(nt, content);
   103	        touch(nt);
   104	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   105	        return;
   106	      }
   107	      // Directory rename (atomic move of the node and all descendants) — the
   108	      // lock's dead-reclaim arbitration renames the stale lock dir aside.
   109	      if (dirs.has(nf)) {
   110	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   111	        const pre = nf + '/';
   112	        dirs.delete(nf);
   113	        dirs.add(nt);
   114	        for (const k of [...store.keys()]) {
   115	          if (k.startsWith(pre)) {
   116	            store.set(nt + k.slice(nf.length), store.get(k));
   117	            store.delete(k);
   118	          }
   119	        }
   120	        for (const d of [...dirs]) {
   121	          if (d !== nt && d.startsWith(pre)) {
   122	            dirs.add(nt + d.slice(nf.length));
   123	            dirs.delete(d);
   124	          }
   125	        }
   126	        touch(nt);
   127	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   128	        return;
   129	      }
   130	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rename', nf);
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    mkdirSync(p, opts = {}) {
   134	      const np = normalize(p);
   135	      if (opts.recursive) {
   136	        mkdirp(np);
   137	        record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   138	        return undefined;
   139	      }
   140	      ensureParent(np, 'mkdir');
   141	      if (dirs.has(np) || store.has(np)) throw fsError('EEXIST', 'mkdir', np);
   142	      dirs.add(np);
   143	      record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   144	      return undefined;
   145	    },
   146	
   147	    existsSync(p) {
   148	      const np = normalize(p);
   149	      return store.has(np) || dirs.has(np);
   150	    },
   151	
   152	    readdirSync(p) {
   153	      const np = normalize(p);
   154	      if (!dirs.has(np)) {
   155	        if (store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOTDIR', 'scandir', np);
   156	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'scandir', np);
   157	      }
   158	      const names = new Set();
   159	      const collect = (k) => {
   160	        if (k !== np && dirname(k) === np) names.add(basename(k, np));
   161	      };
   162	      for (const k of store.keys()) collect(k);
   163	      for (const d of dirs) collect(d);
   164	      return [...names];
   165	    },
   166	
   167	    unlinkSync(p) {
   168	      const np = normalize(p);
   169	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'unlink', np);
   170	      store.delete(np);
   171	      record('unlinkSync', [np]);
   172	    },
   173	
   174	    rmSync(p, opts = {}) {
   175	      const np = normalize(p);
   176	      const isFile = store.has(np);
   177	      const isDir = dirs.has(np);
   178	      if (!isFile && !isDir) {
   179	        if (opts.force) return;
   180	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rm', np);
   181	      }
   182	      if (isFile) store.delete(np);
   183	      if (isDir) {
   184	        const pre = np + '/';
   185	        if (!opts.recursive) {
   186	          const hasChildren = [...store.keys(), ...dirs].some((k) => k !== np && k.startsWith(pre));
   187	          if (hasChildren) throw fsError('ENOTEMPTY', 'rmdir', np);
   188	          dirs.delete(np);
   189	        } else {
   190	          for (const k of [...store.keys()]) if (k === np || k.startsWith(pre)) store.delete(k);
   191	          for (const d of [...dirs]) if (d === np || d.startsWith(pre)) dirs.delete(d);
   192	        }
   193	      }
   194	      record('rmSync', [np]);
   195	    },
   196	
   197	    statSync(p) {
   198	      const np = normalize(p);
   199	      if (store.has(np)) {
   200	        const size = Buffer.byteLength(store.get(np));
   201	        return { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   202	      }
   203	      if (dirs.has(np)) {
   204	        return { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size: 0, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   205	      }
   206	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'stat', np);
   207	    },
   208	
   209	    // The fake cannot represent symlinks, so lstat === stat and realpath is
   210	    // normalization. Symlink-refusal behavior is proven over the real fs in
   211	    // tests/integration/symlink-escape.test.mjs.
   212	    lstatSync(p) {
   213	      return fs.statSync(p);
   214	    },
   215	
   216	    realpathSync(p) {
   217	      const np = normalize(p);
   218	      if (!store.has(np) && !dirs.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'realpath', np);
   219	      return np;
   220	    },
   221	
   222	    copyFileSync(src, dest) {
   223	      const ns = normalize(src);
   224	      const nd = normalize(dest);
   225	      if (!store.has(ns)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'copyfile', ns);
   226	      ensureParent(nd, 'copyfile');
   227	      store.set(nd, store.get(ns));
   228	      touch(nd);
   229	      record('copyFileSync', [ns, nd]);
   230	    },
   231	  };
   232	
   233	  // Introspection hook for atomicity assertions; not part of the real fs API.
   234	  fs.__history = history;
   235	
   236	  return { fs, files: () => snapshot() };
   237	}

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba core/src/scaffold/gitignore.mjs | sed -n '1,120p'" in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Ensure an ignore pattern is active in a .gitignore body. Pure text
     3	 * transform: a covering non-comment line ('.handoff/' or '.handoff') makes
     4	 * this a byte-for-byte no-op; otherwise the pattern is appended on its own
     5	 * line with a single trailing newline.
     6	 * @param {string | null} existingText current .gitignore body, or null when absent
     7	 * @param {string} pattern the ignore entry to guarantee (e.g. '.handoff/')
     8	 * @returns {{text: string, changed: boolean}}
     9	 */
    10	export function ensureIgnoreLine(existingText, pattern) {
    11	  const body = existingText ?? '';
    12	  const bare = pattern.endsWith('/') ? pattern.slice(0, -1) : pattern;
    13	  const covers = body
    14	    .split(/\r?\n/)
    15	    .map((l) => l.trim())
    16	    .some((l) => l === pattern || l === bare);
    17	  if (covers) return { text: body, changed: false };
    18	
    19	  if (body === '') return { text: pattern + '\n', changed: true };
    20	  const joined = body.endsWith('\n') ? body : body + '\n';
    21	  return { text: joined + pattern + '\n', changed: true };
    22	}

     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { ensureIgnoreLine } from '../../core/src/scaffold/gitignore.mjs';
     4	
     5	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     6	// Contract choices for core/src/scaffold/gitignore.mjs (docs/design/core.md
     7	// §file tree "gitignore.mjs — ensure-line (pure text transform)"; §Per-module
     8	// test lists "gitignore variants (covering pattern no-op, CRLF, trailing
     9	// newline, idempotent double-run)"; plan §Scaffold ".gitignore ensure-line
    10	// (no-op if an existing pattern covers .handoff/)").
    11	//
    12	// TARGET MODULE: core/src/scaffold/gitignore.mjs (sole target — its absence is
    13	// the only reason this file is RED).
    14	//
    15	// SIGNATURE (pinned — the design names the module but not the function):
    16	//   ensureIgnoreLine(existingText: string | null, pattern: string)
    17	//     -> {text: string, changed: boolean}
    18	//   PURE (text in, text out; no fs, no io). `existingText` is the current
    19	//   .gitignore body, or null when the file does not exist yet. `pattern` is the
    20	//   ignore entry to guarantee (the scaffolder passes ".handoff/").
    21	//
    22	// PINS (where the design left room):
    23	//   G1. COVERING NO-OP. If a NON-COMMENT line, trimmed, already covers the
    24	//       pattern, the function is a no-op: text === existingText byte-for-byte and
    25	//       changed:false. "Covers .handoff/" is satisfied by an existing line equal
    26	//       to the pattern itself (".handoff/") OR to the directory without the
    27	//       trailing slash (".handoff") — git treats both as ignoring the directory,
    28	//       so re-adding is redundant. (A commented-out "# .handoff/" does NOT count
    29	//       as covering.)
    30	//   G2. APPEND WHEN ABSENT. Otherwise the pattern is appended on its own line and
    31	//       changed:true. The result ALWAYS ends with a single trailing newline, and
    32	//       a non-empty prior body is separated from the new line by exactly one
    33	//       newline (no blank-line gap, no lost final line). Every byte of the prior
    34	//       body is preserved (the appended text starts with the original content).
    35	//   G3. NULL / EMPTY. existingText null or "" -> text is exactly the pattern plus
    36	//       a trailing newline, changed:true.
    37	//   G4. CRLF. A body using CRLF line endings is parsed on CRLF for the covering
    38	//       check (a "\r\n"-terminated ".handoff/" line still counts as covering, G1),
    39	//       and no line is duplicated. (The appended-line ending style is left to the
    40	//       implementer; these tests assert the covering no-op and non-duplication,
    41	//       not the CRLF byte of the appended line.)
    42	//   G5. IDEMPOTENT. ensureIgnoreLine(ensureIgnoreLine(x, p).text, p).changed is
    43	//       false and its text is byte-identical to the first result — a double run
    44	//       adds nothing.
    45	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    46	
    47	const P = '.handoff/';
    48	
    49	// ===========================================================================
    50	describe('scaffold/gitignore.ensureIgnoreLine — appends when the pattern is absent', () => {
    51	  it('null input -> exactly the pattern + trailing newline, changed:true', () => {
    52	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(null, P);
    53	    assert.equal(r.text, `${P}\n`);
    54	    assert.equal(r.changed, true);
    55	  });
    56	
    57	  it('empty input -> exactly the pattern + trailing newline, changed:true', () => {
    58	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine('', P);
    59	    assert.equal(r.text, `${P}\n`);
    60	    assert.equal(r.changed, true);
    61	  });
    62	
    63	  it('appends to an unrelated body, preserving every prior byte and ending in one newline', () => {
    64	    const existing = 'node_modules/\ncoverage/\n*.log\n';
    65	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
    66	    assert.equal(r.changed, true);
    67	    assert.ok(r.text.startsWith(existing), 'the prior body is preserved byte-for-byte as a prefix');
    68	    assert.match(r.text, /(^|\n)\.handoff\/\n$/, 'the pattern lands on its own final line with a single trailing newline');
    69	    assert.ok(!r.text.endsWith('\n\n'), 'no blank-line gap is introduced');
    70	  });
    71	
    72	  it('appends correctly when the prior body lacks a trailing newline (no line joined)', () => {
    73	    const existing = 'node_modules/\n*.log'; // no final newline
    74	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
    75	    assert.equal(r.changed, true);
    76	    const lines = r.text.replace(/\n$/, '').split('\n');
    77	    assert.ok(lines.includes('*.log'), 'the un-terminated final line is not merged into the new one');
    78	    assert.ok(lines.includes('.handoff/'), 'the pattern is present on its own line');
    79	  });
    80	});
    81	
    82	// ===========================================================================
    83	describe('scaffold/gitignore.ensureIgnoreLine — covering no-op', () => {
    84	  it('an exact ".handoff/" line already present -> byte-identical no-op', () => {
    85	    const existing = 'node_modules/\n.handoff/\n*.log\n';
    86	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
    87	    assert.equal(r.changed, false, 'an existing covering line means no change');
    88	    assert.equal(r.text, existing, 'the body is returned byte-for-byte unchanged');
    89	  });
    90	
    91	  it('a ".handoff" line without the trailing slash still covers the directory (no-op)', () => {
    92	    const existing = '.handoff\nnode_modules/\n';
    93	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
    94	    assert.equal(r.changed, false, '".handoff" ignores the directory, so ".handoff/" is redundant');
    95	    assert.equal(r.text, existing);
    96	  });
    97	
    98	  it('a COMMENTED-out "# .handoff/" does NOT count as covering (the pattern is still appended)', () => {
    99	    const existing = '# .handoff/\nnode_modules/\n';
   100	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
   101	    assert.equal(r.changed, true, 'a comment is not an active ignore rule');
   102	    assert.ok(r.text.startsWith(existing), 'the comment body is preserved');
   103	    assert.match(r.text, /(^|\n)\.handoff\/\n$/);
   104	  });
   105	});
   106	
   107	// ===========================================================================
   108	describe('scaffold/gitignore.ensureIgnoreLine — CRLF handling', () => {
   109	  it('a CRLF body already containing ".handoff/" is a no-op (line not duplicated)', () => {
   110	    const existing = 'node_modules/\r\n.handoff/\r\n*.log\r\n';
   111	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
   112	    assert.equal(r.changed, false, 'the covering check must tolerate CRLF endings');
   113	    assert.equal(r.text, existing, 'a CRLF body that already covers is returned unchanged');
   114	  });
   115	
   116	  it('a CRLF body WITHOUT the pattern appends it exactly once', () => {
   117	    const existing = 'node_modules/\r\n*.log\r\n';
   118	    const r = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P);
   119	    assert.equal(r.changed, true);
   120	    const occurrences = r.text.split(/\r?\n/).filter((l) => l.trim() === '.handoff/').length;
   121	    assert.equal(occurrences, 1, 'the pattern appears exactly once, never duplicated');
   122	    assert.ok(r.text.startsWith(existing), 'the prior CRLF body is preserved byte-for-byte');
   123	  });
   124	});
   125	
   126	// ===========================================================================
   127	describe('scaffold/gitignore.ensureIgnoreLine — idempotency and purity', () => {
   128	  it('a double run adds nothing and is byte-identical', () => {
   129	    const first = ensureIgnoreLine('node_modules/\n', P);
   130	    assert.equal(first.changed, true);
   131	    const second = ensureIgnoreLine(first.text, P);
   132	    assert.equal(second.changed, false, 'the second run finds the pattern already covering');
   133	    assert.equal(second.text, first.text, 'the second run is byte-identical (idempotent)');
   134	  });
   135	
   136	  it('is deterministic across repeated calls', () => {
   137	    const existing = 'node_modules/\n';
   138	    assert.equal(ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P).text, ensureIgnoreLine(existing, P).text);
   139	  });
   140	});

   127	    24	//   selfHealWorktree(root, {seat, branch}, io) - Promise<{ok, healed}>
   128	    25	//   teardownWorktrees(root, io)              - Promise<{ok, removed:[…]}>
   129	    26	//   deleteBranch(root, branch, io)           - Promise<{ok:true} | {ok:false, error}> (branch jail)
   130	    27	//
   131	    28	// RED MECHANISM: dynamic-import-with-catch + M() guard (meaningful reds).

   147	    44	const EMAIL = 'ssakshamu@gmail.com';
   148	    45	const MAIN_SHA = 'aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa';
   149	    46	
   150	    47	// A recording fake git. `responses` maps an args-substring (longest match wins)
   151	    48	// to a canned result; { reject:true } models a nonzero git exit.
   152	    49	function fakeGit(responses = {}) {
   153	    50	  const keys = Object.keys(responses).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length);
   154	    51	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
   155	    52	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
   156	    53	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
   157	    54	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd });
   158	    55	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   159	    56	    const k = keys.find((key) => argstr.includes(key));
   160	    57	    const r = k ? responses[k] : null;
   161	    58	    if (r?.reject) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(r.stderr || 'git failed'), { code: r.code ?? 1, stdout: r.stdout ?? '', stderr: r.stderr ?? '' }));
   162	    59	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: r?.stdout ?? '', stderr: r?.stderr ?? '' });
   163	    60	  };
   164	    61	  fn.calls = calls;
   165	    62	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
   166	    63	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   167	    64	  return fn;
   168	    65	}
   169	    66	
   170	    67	// git worktree list --porcelain output for the given seats.
   171	    68	function porcelain(entries) {
   172	    69	  return entries
   173	    70	    .map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${e.sha ?? MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`)
   174	    71	    .join('\n');
   175	    72	}
   176	    73	
   177	    74	function makeRepo({ git, files = {} } = {}) {
   178	    75	  const io = makeIo({
   179	    76	    now: '2026-07-19T00:00:00.000Z',
   180	    77	    host: 'wt-host',
   181	    78	    pid: 4242,
   182	    79	    startTime: 111000,
   183	    80	    files: { '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n', ...files },
   184	    81	  });
   185	    82	  if (git) io.execFile = git;
   186	    83	  io.__git = git;
   187	    84	  return io;
   188	    85	}
   189	    86	
   190	    87	// A null-delimited `git log` line per commit for the attribution scan.
   191	    88	const logLine = (sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body) => [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body].join(' ');
   192	    89

    35	/** @param {any} text @returns {string | null} */
    36	const parseVersion = (text) => String(text).match(/\d+(?:\.\d+)+/)?.[0] ?? null;

    38	/** Numeric segment-wise version compare: negative when a < b. */
    39	function cmpVersions(/** @type {string} */ a, /** @type {string} */ b) {
    40	  const as = a.split('.').map(Number);
    41	  const bs = b.split('.').map(Number);
    42	  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(as.length, bs.length); i++) {
    43	    const d = (as[i] ?? 0) - (bs[i] ?? 0);
    44	    if (d !== 0) return d < 0 ? -1 : 1;
    45	  }
    46	  return 0;
    47	}

    49	/** @param {string} platform @param {any} io @returns {Promise<string | null>} */
    50	async function versionOf(platform, io) {
    51	  try {
    52	    const r = await io.execFile(VERSION_BINS[platform], ['--version'], { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
    53	    return parseVersion(`${r.stdout ?? ''} ${r.stderr ?? ''}`);
    54	  } catch {
    55	    return null;
    56	  }
    57	}

    59	/** @param {string} platform @param {string | null} v @returns {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail: string}} */
    60	function versionCheck(platform, v) {
    61	  const id = `version-${platform}`;
    62	  if (v === null) {
    63	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${VERSION_BINS[platform]} version unverifiable (not installed or --version unsupported) — floor not enforced` };
    64	  }
    65	  const f = FLOORS[platform];
    66	  if (f.floor && cmpVersions(v, f.floor) < 0) {
    67	    return { id, ok: false, detail: `${platform} ${v} is below the supported floor ${f.label} — lifecycle hooks unavailable; the adapter degrades to protocol-only (Tier C)` };
    68	  }
    69	  if (cmpVersions(v, TESTED[platform]) > 0) {
    70	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} is newer than tested (${TESTED[platform]}) — untested; signature drift is absorbed by the overlay table` };
    71	  }
    72	  return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} (tested ${TESTED[platform]})` };
    73	}

    75	/**
    76	 * Recursively collect every `command`/`commandWindows` STRING under a hook
    77	 * definition (codex nests hooks[].hooks[].command; cursor is [].command). Only
    78	 * real command fields — never matchers, comments, or serialized metadata — so
    79	 * enablement keys on an actual invocation (iter-5 A3).
    80	 * @param {any} def @returns {string[]}
    81	 */
    82	function hookCommands(def) {
    83	  /** @type {string[]} */
    84	  const out = [];
    85	  const walk = (/** @type {any} */ node) => {
    86	    if (Array.isArray(node)) {
    87	      for (const n of node) walk(n);
    88	    } else if (node && typeof node === 'object') {
    89	      if (typeof node.command === 'string') out.push(node.command);
    90	      if (typeof node.commandWindows === 'string') out.push(node.commandWindows);
    91	      if (Array.isArray(node.hooks)) walk(node.hooks);
    92	    }
    93	  };
    94	  walk(def);
    95	  return out;
    96	}

   230	async function gitGuardCheck(io, root) {
   231	  let log;
   232	  try {
   233	    log = await io.execFile('git', ['log', '-n', String(GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND), '--format=%h %s%n%n%b'], { cwd: root, timeout: 5000 });
   234	  } catch {
   235	    return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: 'git unavailable — commit scan skipped' };
   236	  }
   237	  // Chunks start at a line beginning with an abbreviated sha + space.
   238	  const chunks = log.stdout.split(/^(?=[0-9a-f]{7,40} )/m).filter((/** @type {string} */ c) => c.trim() !== '');
   239	  for (const chunk of chunks) {
   240	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(chunk))) {
   241	      const sha = chunk.slice(0, chunk.indexOf(' '));

   246	      };
   247	    }
   248	  }
   249	  return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: `no AI trailers in the last ${GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND} commits` };

   253	 * `baton doctor` — machine health report: attribution settings, git-guard
   254	 * trailer scan, journal size, filesystem boundary, and per-platform probe

   260	export async function cmdDoctor(args, io) {
   261	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { strict: 'boolean' });
   262	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'doctor', parsed.error);

   193	function readOrNull(io, path) {

   201	/**
   202	 * Classify one probe invocation per the two-dimension record: capability is
   203	 * the ordered ladder verified as far as the evidence goes; outcome is what the
   204	 * probe itself did. A server-issued limit response proves authentication.
   205	 * @param {{platform: string, bin: string, args: string[], capOnSuccess?: string}} probe @param {any} io @param {any} table
   206	 */
   207	async function runProbe(probe, io, table) {
   208	  try {
   209	    await io.execFile(probe.bin, probe.args, { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
   210	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: probe.capOnSuccess ?? 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' };
   211	  } catch (err) {
   212	    const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   213	    if (e?.code === 'ENOENT') return { platform: probe.platform, capability: null, outcome: 'error' };
   214	    if (e?.killed === true) return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'timeout' };
   215	    const text = `${e?.stdout ?? ''}\n${e?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   216	    if (table && classify({ text, exitCode: 1, platform: probe.platform, table, structured: null }).class === 'usage-limit') {
   217	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' };
   218	    }

     6	
     7	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     8	// Command-level contract for core/src/commands/doctor.mjs. Direct import over a
     9	// fakeio whose execFile is REPLACED with a responder (doctor probes platforms
    10	// and scans git via the async injected execFile, so cmdDoctor is async).
    11	//
    12	// TARGET MODULE: core/src/commands/doctor.mjs (sole target — its absence is the
    13	// only reason this file is RED). doctor internally consumes git/guard.mjs,
    14	// git/snapshot.mjs and the probe layer; this file imports ONLY doctor and drives
    15	// everything through it (git/guard.mjs is NOT a Wave-D target, so it is exercised
    16	// through doctor, never imported here).
    17	//
    18	// Source of truth: plan §Role matrix "Availability probing" (doctor probes each
    19	// platform noninteractively, HARD 3s bound per probe, results cached 15 min;
    20	// probe record = two SEPARATE dimensions: capability {installed→authenticated→
    21	// reachable} recorded as far as verifiable, and outcome {ok|rate-limited|error|
    22	// timeout}); §Attribution guard layer 2 (doctor scans the LAST 50 COMMITS for AI
    23	// co-author/generated-with trailers, WORD-BOUNDARY-safe, and flags with
    24	// remediation); §"Journal rotation" (doctor warns at journal > 5 MB);
    25	// §"Filesystem support boundary" (doctor detects + warns on network/shared
    26	// mounts); §CLI contract (--strict exit).
    27	//
    28	// PINS (where the spec left the command surface open — the implementer conforms):
    29	//   D1. SIGNATURE: cmdDoctor(args, io) -> Promise<number>. --json emits exactly
    30	//       one compact {ok,data,warnings,error} envelope on stdout.
    31	//   D2. --json data carries CHECKS and per-platform PROBE RECORDS. Shape is read
    32	//       tolerantly (array of {id, ok, detail?} OR a map for checks; array of
    33	//       {platform, capability, outcome, ...} OR a map for platforms), so the
    34	//       exact container is not over-pinned; what IS pinned:
    35	//         - a check whose id matches /attribution/ reflects the .claude/
    36	//           settings.json attribution state (ok when commit==="" && pr==="");
    37	//         - a check whose id matches /guard|commit|trailer/ is the AI-trailer
    38	//           commit scan;
    39	//         - a check whose id matches /journal/ warns when the journal > 5 MB;
    40	//         - a check whose id matches /filesystem|network|mount|fs/ exists;
    41	//         - EVERY platform record exposes BOTH dimensions: `capability` ∈
    42	//           {installed, authenticated, reachable} (the ordered cumulative
    43	//           ladder; verifier fold F5) and `outcome` ∈ {ok, rate-limited, error,
    44	//           timeout}. A not-installed platform may fall below the ladder; every
    45	//           INSTALLED platform's capability must be one of the three rungs.
    46	//   D3. PROBES ARE TIME-BOUNDED: every probe (non-git) execFile call is invoked
    47	//       with an options object carrying timeout <= 3000 (the hard 3 s bound),
    48	//       mirroring git/snapshot.mjs's timeout:5000 convention.
    49	//   D4. PROBES ARE CACHED ~15 min: a SECOND doctor run within the window makes
    50	//       ZERO new probe (non-git) execFile calls (cache reuse via io.fs), while a
    51	//       run AFTER the window re-probes. (The cache lives under io.fs so it
    52	//       persists across calls; its exact path is left to the implementer.)
    53	//   D5. GIT-GUARD is WORD-BOUNDARY-safe: a commit carrying a "Co-Authored-By:
    54	//       Claude" trailer is flagged (its sha surfaces in the envelope); a history
    55	//       whose only "Claude"-ish token is the name "Claudette Smith" is NOT
    56	//       flagged. doctor scans FULL commit messages (subject + body), where
    57	//       trailers live, and (verifier fold F6) the scan is BOUNDED to the last 50
    58	//       commits — the git log invocation must carry `-n 50` / `-n50` /
    59	//       `--max-count=50` / `--max-count 50` (any of these forms).
    60	//   D6. --strict EXIT: without --strict doctor is report-only (exit 0 even with
    61	//       findings); with --strict a failing check (e.g. a flagged AI trailer)
    62	//       exits non-zero (1). An all-green machine exits 0 either way.
    63	//   D7. PROBE STAGING (verifier fold F5 — the fixture oracle, pinned here since
    64	//       the plan names the dimensions but not the probe wire protocol):
    65	//       doctor probes each platform by invoking its CLI binary noninteractively
    66	//       via io.execFile — `claude` for claude-code, `codex` for codex,
    67	//       `cursor-agent` for cursor (the local CLIs named in docs/design/
    68	//       platform-notes.md; multiple calls per binary are permitted, all answered
    69	//       identically by the fixture). The probe result is classified from the
    70	//       invocation result:
    71	//         - clean resolution                        -> capability 'reachable', outcome 'ok'
    72	//         - output matching /not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i
    73	//                                                   -> capability 'installed' (auth
    74	//                                                      unverifiable), outcome != 'ok'
    75	//         - output matching the platform's limit signatures (signatures.v1.json,
    76	//           e.g. "You've hit your usage limit")     -> outcome 'rate-limited'
    77	//           (a server-issued limit response also PROVES auth: capability is at
    78	//           least 'authenticated')
    79	//         - output with auth evidence (/authenticated as|logged in as/i) plus a
    80	//           network failure (/unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i)
    81	//                                                   -> capability 'authenticated',
    82	//                                                      outcome 'error'
    83	//         - a rejection shaped like an execFile timeout kill (err.killed === true)
    84	//                                                   -> outcome 'timeout'
    85	//         - any other rejection                     -> outcome 'error'

   112	/** Answer one probe invocation according to its staged behavior kind (D7). */
   113	function respondProbe(kind, resolve, reject) {
   114	  switch (kind) {
   115	    case 'ok':
   116	      return resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   117	    case 'auth-fail': {
   118	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   119	      e.code = 1;
   120	      e.stdout = '';
   121	      e.stderr = 'Error: not logged in — run the login flow first\n';
   122	      return reject(e);
   123	    }
   124	    case 'net-fail': {
   125	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   126	      e.code = 1;
   127	      e.stdout = '';
   128	      e.stderr = 'Authenticated as user@example.test, but the service is unreachable (ECONNREFUSED)\n';
   129	      return reject(e);
   130	    }
   131	    case 'rate-limited': {
   132	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   133	      e.code = 1;
   134	      e.stdout = '';
   135	      e.stderr = "You've hit your usage limit. Please try again at 3pm.\n";
   136	      return reject(e);
   137	    }
   138	    case 'timeout': {
   139	      const e = new Error('timed out');
   140	      e.killed = true;
   141	      e.signal = 'SIGTERM';
   142	      return reject(e);
   143	    }
   144	    case 'error': {
   145	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   146	      e.code = 1;
   147	      e.stdout = '';
   148	      e.stderr = 'fatal: unexpected internal error\n';
   149	      return reject(e);
   150	    }
   151	    default:
   152	      return resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   153	  }
   154	}
   155	
   156	/**
   157	 * Build a doctor io: fakeio for fs/stdout, but execFile REPLACED with a
   158	 * responder that answers git subcommands from `gitLog`/clean fixtures and
   159	 * treats every non-git command as a platform probe answered per `probes`
   160	 * (a map of binary basename -> behavior kind; default all 'ok').
   161	 * io.__calls records {cmd, args, opts}.
   162	 */
   163	function makeDoctorIo({ files = {}, gitLog = GIT_LOG_CLEAN, now = NOW, probes = {} } = {}) {
   164	  const io = makeIo({ files, env: { HOME: '/home/user' }, now });
   165	  const calls = [];
   166	  io.__calls = calls;
   167	  io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
   168	    calls.push({ cmd, args: [...args], opts });
   169	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
   170	      if (cmd === 'git') {
   171	        const sub = args[0];
   172	        if (sub === 'log') return resolve({ stdout: gitLog, stderr: '' });
   173	        if (sub === 'rev-parse') return resolve({ stdout: args.includes('HEAD') && !args.includes('--abbrev-ref') ? 'f0f0f0f0\n' : 'main\n', stderr: '' });
   174	        return resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   175	      }
   176	      // Non-git command === a platform probe; behavior keyed on the binary basename.
   177	      const bin = String(cmd).split('/').pop();
   178	      return respondProbe(probes[bin] ?? 'ok', resolve, reject);
   179	    });
   180	  };

   186	/** Parse the single compact --json envelope and check its wire shape. */
   187	function envelope(io) {
   188	  const parsed = JSON.parse(io.stdoutText());
   189	  assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), JSON.stringify(parsed) + '\n', 'stdout is exactly one compact envelope + newline');
   190	  assert.ok('ok' in parsed && 'data' in parsed && 'warnings' in parsed && 'error' in parsed, 'envelope carries ok/data/warnings/error');
   191	  return parsed;
   192	}
   193	
   194	/** Normalize data.checks (array of {id,ok} OR a map) to an array of {id, ok, ...}. */
   195	function checkList(env) {
   196	  const c = env.data && env.data.checks;
   197	  if (Array.isArray(c)) return c;
   198	  if (c && typeof c === 'object') return Object.entries(c).map(([id, v]) => ({ id, ...(v && typeof v === 'object' ? v : { ok: v }) }));
   199	  return [];
   200	}
   201	const findCheck = (env, re) => checkList(env).find((c) => re.test(String(c.id)));
   202	
   203	/** Normalize data.platforms (array OR map) to an array of records. */
   204	function platformRecords(env) {
   205	  const p = env.data && env.data.platforms;
   206	  if (Array.isArray(p)) return p;
   207	  if (p && typeof p === 'object') return Object.entries(p).map(([platform, v]) => ({ platform, ...(v && typeof v === 'object' ? v : {}) }));
   208	  return [];
   209	}
   210	const recordFor = (env, platform) => platformRecords(env).find((r) => r.platform === platform);

[high] — Fake git is too permissive for adversarial command checking: responses are selected by args substring [tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs:56), unknown git commands succeed with empty output [tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs:59), and destructive-absence checks use narrow regexes such as `branch -D` only [tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs:402). Hypothesis: an author-only scanner, or a scanner issuing default `git log`, could pass these tests. — Fix: add a foreign-committer-only case, assert the `git log` args include an explicit null-delimited format carrying sha/author/committer/body, and add generated-with/by/emoji trailer positives from the doctor patterns [core/src/commands/doctor.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/doctor.mjs:185).

[high] — Fake git is too permissive for adversarial command checking: responses are selected by args substring [tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs:56), unknown git commands succeed with empty output [tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs:59), and destructive-absence checks use narrow regexes such as `branch -D` only [tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-worktrees.test.mjs:402). Hypothesis: `git branch -d feature/important`, `git clean -fd`, or reordered/destructive commands could slip past refusal tests. — Fix: make unstubbed git calls reject, match expected args structurally, and add a shared `assertNoDestructiveGit(calls)` over the full recorded list covering branch `-d/-D`, reset, clean, checkout/restore, rebase, merge, and worktree remove where prohibited.

Hypothesis: syncing the main cwd twice, or the same worktree twice, would pass. — Fix: assert exactly one ff-only sync in `/repo/.worktrees/wt-a` and one in `/repo/.worktrees/wt-b`, and assert pre/postflight `status`/`rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` run in the target seat cwd.
